\\n93: struct DataPartsLock\\n94: {\\n95:     std::optional<Stopwatch> wait_watch;\\n96:     std::unique_lock<std::mutex> lock;\\n97:     std::optional<Stopwatch> lock_watch;\\n98:     DataPartsLock() = default;\\n99:     explicit DataPartsLock(std::mutex & data_parts_mutex_);\\n100: \\n101:     ~DataPartsLock();\\n102: };\\n103: \\n104: /// Data structure for *MergeTree engines.\\n105: /// Merge tree is used for incremental sorting of data.\\n106: /// The table consists of several sorted parts.\\n107: /// During insertion new data is sorted according to the primary key and is written to the new part.\\n108: /// Parts are merged in the background according to a heuristic algorithm.\\n109: /// For each part the index file is created containing primary key values for every n-th row.\\n110: /// This allows efficient selection by primary key range predicate.\\n111: ///\\n112: /// Additionally:\\n113: ///\\n114: /// The date column is specified. For each part min and max dates are remembered.\\n115: /// Essentially it is an index too.\\n116: ///\\n117: /// Data is partitioned by the value of the partitioning expression.\\n118: /// Parts belonging to different partitions are not merged - for the ease of administration (data sync and backup).\\n119: ///\\n120: /// File structure of old-style month-partitioned tables (format_version = 0):\\n121: /// Part directory - / min-date _ max-date _ min-id _ max-id _ level /\\n122: /// Inside the part directory:\\n123: /// checksums.txt - contains the list of all files along with their sizes and checksums.\\n124: /// columns.txt - contains the list of all columns and their types.\\n125: /// primary.idx - contains the primary index.\\n126: /// [Column].bin - contains compressed column data.\\n127: /// [Column].mrk - marks, pointing to seek positions allowing to skip n * k rows.\\n128: ///\\n129: /// File structure of tables with custom partitioning (format_version >= 1):\\n130: /// Part directory - / partition-id _ min-id _ max-id _ level /\\n131: /// Inside the part directory:\\n132: /// The same files as for month-partitioned tables, plus\\n133: /// count.txt - contains total number of rows in this part.\\n134: /// partition.dat - contains the value of the partitioning expression.\\n135: /// minmax_[Column].idx - MinMax indexes (see IMergeTreeDataPart::MinMaxIndex class) for the columns required by the partitioning expression.\\n136: ///\\n137: /// Several modes are implemented. Modes determine additional actions during merge:\\n138: /// - Ordinary - don't do anything special\\n139: /// - Collapsing - collapse pairs of rows with the opposite values of sign_columns for the same values\\n140: ///   of primary key (cf. CollapsingSortedTransform.h)\\n141: /// - Replacing - for all rows with the same primary key keep only the latest one. Or, if the version\\n142: ///   column is set, keep the latest row with the maximal version.\\n143: /// - Summing - sum all numeric columns not contained in the primary key for all rows with the same primary key.\\n144: /// - Aggregating - merge columns containing aggregate function states for all rows with the same primary key.\\n145: /// - Graphite - performs coarsening of historical data for Graphite (a system for quantitative monitoring).\\n146: \\n147: /// The MergeTreeData class contains a list of parts and the data structure parameters.\\n148: /// To read and modify the data use other classes:\\n149: /// - MergeTreeDataSelectExecutor\\n150: /// - MergeTreeDataWriter\\n151: /// - MergeTreeDataMergerMutator\\n152: \\n153: class MergeTreeData : public IStorage, public WithMutableContext\\n154: {\\n155: public:\\n156:     /// Function to call if the part is suspected to contain corrupt data.\\n157:     using BrokenPartCallback = std::function<void (const String &)>;\\n158:     using DataPart = IMergeTreeDataPart;\\n159: \\n160:     using MutableDataPartPtr = std::shared_ptr<DataPart>;\\n161:     using MutableDataPartsVector = std::vector<MutableDataPartPtr>;\\n162:     /// After the DataPart is added to the working set, it cannot be changed.\\n163:     using DataPartPtr = std::shared_ptr<const DataPart>;\\n164: \\n165:     using DataPartState = MergeTreeDataPartState;\\n166:     using DataPartStates = std::initializer_list<DataPartState>;\\n167:     using DataPartStateVector = std::vector<DataPartState>;\\n168: \\n169:     using PinnedPartUUIDsPtr = std::shared_ptr<const PinnedPartUUIDs>;\\n170: \\n171:     constexpr static auto FORMAT_VERSION_FILE_NAME = \\\"format_version.txt\\\";\\n172:     constexpr static auto DETACHED_DIR_NAME = \\\"detached\\\";\\n173:     constexpr static auto MOVING_DIR_NAME = \\\"moving\\\";\\n174: \\n175:     /// Auxiliary structure for index comparison. Keep in mind lifetime of MergeTreePartInfo.\\n176:     struct DataPartStateAndInfo\\n177:     {\\n178:         DataPartState state;\\n179:         const MergeTreePartInfo & info;\\n180:     };\\n181: wants to merge some really big parts into a bigger part,\\n66:  * then it will be executed for a long time, because the result of the merge is not really needed immediately.\\n67:  * It is better to It uses an append-only design where new data is written as immutable sorted parts, with background merge processes combining parts over time. The architecture is optimized for analytics workloads with column-oriented storage, sophisticated indexing, and intelligent query optimization.\n\n---\n\n## 1. High-Level Architecture Diagram\n\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│                     ClickHouse MergeTree                        │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n\n┌─────────────────┐  ┌──────────────────┐  ┌──────────────────────┐\n│   INSERT Path   │  │    MERGE Path    │  │     SELECT Path      │\n│                 │  │                  │  │                      │\n│ ┌─────────────┐ │  │ ┌──────────────┐ │  │ ┌──────────────────┐ │\n│ │  Parser &   │ │  │ │  Merge       │ │  │ │ Query Planner    │ │\n│ │  Optimizer  │ │  │ │  Selector    │ │  │ │ & Optimizer      │ │\n│ └──────┬──────┘ │  │ └──────┬───────┘ │  │ └────────┬─────────┘ │\n│        │        │  │        │         │  │         │           │\n│ ┌──────▼──────┐ │  │ ┌──────▼──────┐ │  │ ┌───────▼──────────┐ │\n│ │  Data       │ │  │ │  Merge      │ │  │ │ Partition &      │ │\n│ │  Writer    │ │  │ │  Algorithm  │ │  │ │ Mark Pruning     │ │\n│ └──────┬──────┘ │  │ └──────┬──────┘ │  │ └────────┬─────────┘ │\n│        │        │  │        │        │  │         │           │\n└────────┼────────┘  └────────┼────────┘  └─────────┼───────────┘\n         │                    │                     │\n         │                    │                     │\n      ┌──▼────────────────────▼─────────────────────▼────┐\n      │        Active Data Part Set (Metadata)            │\n      │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │\n      │  │  Part Info: partition_id, min/max_block,    │ │\n      │  │  level, mutation version                    │ │\n      │  │  • Controls which parts are visible         │ │\n      │  │  • Tracks part hierarchy and versioning    │ │\n      │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │\n      └──┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n         │\n    ┌────▼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n    │                    Data Parts on Disk                        │\n    │                                                               │\n    │  Part 1: [partition_0_1_5_2]  Part 2: [partition_0_6_10_2] │\n    │  ┌──────────────────────────┐  ┌──────────────────────────┐ │\n    │  │ columns.txt              │  │ columns.txt              │ │\n    │  │ primary.idx              │  │ primary.idx              │ │\n    │  │ skp_idx_*.idx (indices)  │  │ skp_idx_*.idx (indices)  │ │\n    │  │ column1.bin/mrk*         │  │ column1.bin/mrk*         │ │\n    │  │ column2.bin/mrk*         │  │ column2.bin/mrk*         │ │\n    │  │ checksums.txt            │  │ checksums.txt            │ │\n    │  │ partition.dat            │  │ partition.dat            │ │\n    │  └──────────────────────────┘  └──────────────────────────┘ │\n    │                                                               │\n    └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n---\n\n## 2. Core Architecture Components\n\n### 2.1 MergeTreeData - Central Orchestrator\n**File**: `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeData.h`\n\nMergeTreeData is the central class managing the entire storage engine:\n\n- **DataPartSet Management**: Maintains active parts with `ActiveDataPartSet` using a sorted map indexed by `MergeTreePartInfo`\n- **Metadata**: Stores table schema, partitioning expression, primary key, and index definitions\n- **Transaction Support**: Manages MVCC with transaction snapshots for consistent reads\n- **Mutex Hierarchy**: Thread-safe part management with `data_parts_mutex`\n- **Background Jobs**: Coordinates merge and mutation tasks through `BackgroundJobsAssignee`\n\n### 2.2 IMergeTreeDataPart - Part Abstraction\n**File**: `src/Storages/MergeTree/IMergeTreeDataPart.h`\n\nBase class for all data part implementations:\n\n- **Part Identity**: Name (e.g., `partition_0_1_5_2`), UUID, modification time\n- **Metadata**: Column list, checksums, index granularity information\n- **State Tracking**: Part state machine (PreActive → Active → Outdated → Deleting)\n- **Reader Factory**: Creates readers for specific column/mark ranges\n- **Concrete Subclasses**:\n  - `MergeTreeDataPartWide`: Wide format (one file per column) - optimal for large parts\n  - `MergeTreeDataPartCompact`: Compact format (all columns in one file) - optimal for small parts\n\n### 2.3 MergeTreePartInfo - Part Naming and Hierarchyy\n**File**: `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreePartInfo.h`\n\nEncodes part hierarchy in its name: `partition_id_min_block_max_block_level_mutation`\n\n- **Partition ID**: Logical partition identifier (e.g., date, month)\n- **Block Range**: [min_block, max_block] identifies rows by generation number\n- **Level**: Merge depth (0=initial part, 1=merged 2 parts, 2+=merged multiple times)\n- **Mutation**: Version number if part contains mutations\n- **Containment Logic**: Method `contains()` determines if one part fully contains another based on block ranges and levels\n\n---\n\n## 3. Write Path: INSERT to Committed Part\n\n### 3.1 Process Flow\n\n```\nINSERT query\n    ↓\nMergeTreeSink (Sink.h)\n    ↓\nMergeTreeDataWriter::splitBlockIntoParts\n    ├─ Partition pruning by partitioning expression\n    ├─ Group rows into blocks per partition\n    └─ Create BlocksWithPartition vector\n    ↓\nMergeTreeDataWriter::writeTempPart\n    ├─ Generate temporary part name (tmp_...)\n    ├─ Create part directory structure\n    ├─ Allocate disk space reservation\n    └─ Build part using MergeTreeDataPartBuilder\n    ↓\nMergedBlockOutputStream (Wide/Compact format)\n    ├─ Write column data in blocks (granules)\n    │   ├─ Column serialization per granule\n    │   └─ Compression applied\n    ├─ Write marks files (data.mrk*, *.mrk)\n    │   └─ Track seek positions for granule boundaries\n    ├─ Build primary index (primary.idx)\n    │   └─ Sample primary key values at mark boundaries\n    └─ Build skip indices (skp_idx_*.idx)\n    ↓\nMetadata Finalization\n    ├─ Compute and write column checksums (checksums.txt)\n    ├─ Write columns manifest (columns.txt)\n    ├─ Write partition value if custom partitioning (partition.dat)\n    └─ Write index granularity info\n    ↓\nCommit to Active Set\n    ├─ Rename tmp_* → partition_min_max_0\n    ├─ Add to ActiveDataPartSet\n    ├─ Update transaction metadata\n    └─ Notify background processes of new part\n    ↓\nTask Complete\n```\n\n### 3.2 Key Classes and Files\n\n| File Path | Role |\n|-----------|------|\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeDataWriter.h` | Main write orchestrator, partition splitting |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeDataWriter.cpp` | Write implementation, temp part creation |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergedBlockOutputStream.h` | Column data and index writing for Wide parts |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergedBlockOutputStream.cpp` | Stream buffer management and compression |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeSink.h` | INSERT sink interface connecting parser to writer |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeDataPartBuilder.h` | Part object construction with metadata |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeDataPartBuilder.cpp` | Builder implementation and part type selection |\n\n### 3.3 Data Part Structure on Disk\n\n**Wide Format (for large parts)**:\n```\npartition_0_1_5_2/\n├── columns.txt              # List of all columns and their types\n├── primary.idx              # Primary key index (compact, sampled at marks)\n├── skp_idx_name1.idx        # Skip index for index \"name1\"\n├── skp_idx_name2.idx        # Skip index for index \"name2\"\n├── column_name1.bin         # Compressed column data\n├── column_name1.mrk3        # Marks for column_name1\n├── column_name2.bin         # Compressed column data\n├── column_name2.mrk3        # Marks for column_name2\n├── checksums.txt            # File checksums for integrity verification\n├── partition.dat            # Partition expression value (optional)\n└── count.txt               # Total row count\n\nMark file (.mrk3) contains:\n  - Compressed block offsets (8 bytes each)\n  - Uncompressed block offsets (8 bytes each)\n  - Row count in granule (4 bytes)\n  For each granule boundary\n```\n\n**Compact Format (for small parts, <10MB)**:\n```\npartition_0_1_5_2/\n├── columns.txt\n├── primary.idx\n├── data.bin                 # All columns in single file\n├── data.mrk3               # Array of marks for all columns\n├── checksums.txt\n└── count.txt\n```\n\n### 3.4 Marks and Granularity\n\n- **Granule**: Logical unit of data (typically 8192 rows in non-adaptive mode)\n- **Mark**: Pair of offsets (compressed position in .bin file, uncompressed position in memory)\n- **Adaptive Granularity**: Adjusts granule size based on row size to maintain consistent granule sizes in bytes\n- **Mark Range**: [mark_id_start, mark_id_end] specifies which granules to read\n\n---\n\n## 4. Merge Path: Combining Parts\n\n### 4.1 Merge Selection Process\n\n```\nBackground Scheduler (MergeTreeBackgroundExecutor)\n    ↓\nMergeTreeDataMergerMutator::selectPartsToMerge\n    ├─ Step 1: Get Candidate Parts\n    │   └─ ActiveDataPartSet::getDataPartsToSelectMergeFrom()\n    │       Filters to visible, non-locked parts\n    │\n    ├─ Step 2: Determine Mergeable Ranges\n    │   └─ getPossibleMergeRanges()\n    │       Groups consecutive parts by partition\n    │       Checks can_merge_callback for conflicts\n    │\n    └─ Step 3: Select Optimal Parts\n        └─ selectPartsToMergeFromRanges()\n            Uses merge selector strategy:\n            ├─ Simple: Merge smallest adjacent parts\n            ├─ TTL-based: Prioritize parts with expired TTL\n            ├─ Balanced: Balance part count and size\n            └─ Graphite: Coarsen time-series data\n\nDecision: SelectPartsDecision (SELECTED | CANNOT_SELECT | NOTHING_TO_MERGE)\n```\n\n### 4.2 FutureMergedMutatedPart - Merge Task Specification\n**File**: `src/Storages/MergeTree/FutureMergedMutatedPart.h`\n\nDescribes the merge operation to perform:\n\n- **Source Parts**: `DataPartsVector parts` - ordered list of parts to merge\n- **Result Info**: `name`, `part_info` with new min/max blocks and incremented `level`\n- **Merge Type**: Regular merge vs. mutation vs. TTL cleanup\n- **Blocking Parts**: Parts that can be removed after merge completes\n\n### 4.3 Merge Execution - MergeTask\n**File**: `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTask.h`\n\nExecutes the actual merge as a coroutine-like task:\n\n```\nMergeTask::execute() (called repeatedly until returns false)\n    ├─ Stage 1: Preparation\n    │   ├─ Create output part directory structure\n    │   ├─ Prepare readers for all input parts\n    │   ├─ Setup merge algorithm (column-wise or block-wise)\n    │   └─ Initialize progress tracking\n    │\n    ├─ Stage 2: Horizontal Merge (combine row sets)\n    │   ├─ Read rows from input parts in sorted order\n    │   ├─ Apply merging logic (e.g., collapse, replace, sum)\n    │   ├─ Handle mutations if needed\n    │   ├─ Write output blocks via MergedBlockOutputStream\n    │   └─ Update merge progress\n    │\n    ├─ Stage 3: Vertical Merge (rebuild indices)\n    │   ├─ Rebuild primary index from merged data\n    │   ├─ Rebuild skip indices\n    │   └─ Verify data integrity with checksums\n    │\n    └─ Stage 4: Commit Merge\n        ├─ Finalize output part (checksums, columns.txt)\n        ├─ Rename from tmp_* to final name\n        ├─ Add to ActiveDataPartSet\n        ├─ Remove source parts\n        └─ Clean up temporary files\n```\n\n### 4.4 Merge Modes (MergeTreeData::MergingParams)\n\n| Mode | Behavior |\n|------|----------|\n| **Ordinary** | Simple concatenation and re-sorting |\n| **Replacing** | Keep only latest row per primary key (by version or insert order) |\n| **Collapsing** | Remove pairs of rows with opposite `sign` column values |\n| **Summing** | Sum numeric non-key columns for each primary key |\n| **Aggregating** | Merge pre-computed aggregate states |\n| **Graphite** | Coarsen time-series data for monitoring systems |\n\n### 4.5 Key Classes and Files\n\n| File Path | Role |\n|-----------|------|\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeDataMergerMutator.h` | Merge selection logic and heuristics |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeDataMergerMutator.cpp` | Implementation of merge selector |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTask.h` | Core merge execution task (coroutine-like) |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTask.cpp` | Merge algorithm stages: horizontal, vertical, commit |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeList.h` | Merge progress tracking and monitoring |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/ActiveDataPartSet.h` | Maintains set of active parts with containment logic |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/ActiveDataPartSet.cpp` | Part set operations and deduplication |\n\n---\n\n## 5. Read Path: SELECT Query Execution\n\n### 5.1 Query Planning and Optimization\n\n```\nSELECT query\n    ↓\nMergeTreeDataSelectExecutor::read()\n    ├─ Create ReadFromMergeTree processor\n    ├─ Select parts covering requested range\n    └─ Return QueryPlan with optimized execution\n    ↓\nPart Pruning (Partition Elimination)\n    ├─ Use partition expressions to eliminate partitions\n    ├─ PartitionPruner evaluates where conditions\n    └─ Keep only matching partitions\n    ↓\nPrimary Key Index Filtering\n    ├─ Convert WHERE clause to KeyCondition\n    ├─ KeyCondition::checkInRange() on part min/max values\n    └─ Determine mark ranges to read per part\n    ↓\nSkip Index Filtering (Secondary Indices)\n    ├─ Evaluate index conditions per granule\n    ├─ Uses IMergeTreeIndexCondition\n    ├─ Types: minmax, bloom filter, set, full-text, etc.\n    └─ Further refine mark ranges\n    ↓\nMark Range Selection\n    ├─ Merge adjacent/overlapping mark ranges\n    ├─ Determine which granules to actually read\n    └─ Pass to reader for column retrieval\n```\n\n### 5.2 MergeTreeDataSelectExecutor\n**File**: `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeDataSelectExecutor.h`\n\nMain query executor:\n\n- **Part Selection**: `read()` chooses which parts to scan based on constraints\n- **Index Application**: `markRangesFromPKRange()` converts primary key condition to granule ranges\n- **Multi-strategy**: Supports parallel reading, in-order reading, and parallel replicas\n- **Analysis Methods**: `estimateNumMarksToRead()` for cost estimation and projection selection\n\n### 5.3 KeyCondition - Primary Key Index\n**File**: `src/Storages/MergeTree/KeyCondition.h`\n\nEvaluates WHERE conditions against primary key ranges:\n\n- **RPN Compilation**: Converts condition DAG to Reverse Polish Notation\n- **Range Checking**: `checkInRange()` evaluates feasibility over [left_key, right_key] ranges\n- **Mask Result**: Returns `BoolMask` with (can_be_true, can_be_false) components\n- **Optimization**: Short-circuit evaluation when condition is always true/false\n- **Limitation**: Only uses prefix of primary key that can short-circuit the condition\n\n### 5.4 Skip Indices - Secondary Index Selection\n**File**: `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeIndices.h`\n\nPluggable secondary indices for further granule filtering:\n\n| Index Type | Purpose | Overhead |\n|-----------|---------|----------|\n| **minmax** | Track min/max per granule | Minimal (always built) |\n| **bloom_filter** | Fast membership testing | Medium (3-4% space) |\n| **set** | Enumerate allowed values | Low (hash set) |\n| **full-text** | Full-text search in text columns | High (inverted index) |\n| **similarity** | Vector similarity search | Very high (graph index) |\n\nIndex granule = one granule of data with one index entry\n\n### 5.5 Reader Interface - Column Data Retrieval\n**File**: `src/Storages/MergeTree/IMergeTreeReader.h`\n\nAbstract reader interface:\n\n```cpp\nreadRows(size_t from_mark, size_t current_task_last_mark,\n         bool continue_reading, size_t max_rows_to_read,\n         Columns & res_columns)\n  ├─ from_mark: starting granule ID\n  ├─ current_task_last_mark: prefetch boundary for async reads\n  ├─ continue_reading: resume from last position or seek\n  └─ max_rows_to_read: limit rows returned\n```\n\nConcrete implementations:\n- **MergeTreeReaderWide**: Reads Wide format (one file per column)\n- **MergeTreeReaderCompact**: Reads Compact format (all columns in one file)\n\n### 5.6 MergeTreeReaderWide - Wide Format Reading\n**File**: `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeReaderWide.h`\n\nOptimized reader for Wide parts:\n\n- **Per-Column Streams**: One `MergeTreeReaderStream` per column\n- **Parallel Column Loading**: Load multiple columns concurrently\n- **Mark Caching**: Cache decompressed mark ranges in memory\n- **Prefetching**: Async prefetch upcoming granules for remote storage\n\nReading process:\n```\nreadRows()\n  ├─ Seek each column's stream to from_mark\n  ├─ Deserialize column data for max_rows_to_read rows\n  ├─ Apply alter conversions if schema changed\n  ├─ Fill missing columns with defaults\n  └─ Return Columns vector\n```\n\n### 5.7 Data Access Hierarchy\n\n```\nColumn Data on Disk (.bin file)\n  ├─ Compression wrapper (e.g., LZ4, ZSTD)\n  ├─ Serialized blocks (per granule)\n  │   ├─ Block header (sizes, counts)\n  │   └─ Serialized values\n  └─ Footer (checksums)\n\nMark File (.mrk3) - Index into .bin\n  ├─ Entry 0: offset to granule 0\n  ├─ Entry 1: offset to granule 1\n  └─ Entry n: offset to granule n\n\nRead Process:\n  1. Load mark file into MarkCache\n  2. Seek to offset for from_mark\n  3. Stream decompression buffer\n  4. Deserialize rows until max_rows_to_read\n```\n\n### 5.8 Key Classes and Files\n\n| File Path | Role |\n|-----------|------|\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeDataSelectExecutor.h` | Query planning and part selection |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeDataSelectExecutor.cpp` | Index filtering and mark range calculation |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/KeyCondition.h` | Primary key condition evaluation |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/KeyCondition.cpp` | RPN compilation and range checking |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/IMergeTreeReader.h` | Reader interface abstraction |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeReaderWide.h` | Wide format reader implementation |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeReaderWide.cpp` | Column-by-column reading logic |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeReaderStream.h` | Single column read buffer with mark caching |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeIndices.h` | Skip index interface and implementations |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/PartitionPruner.h` | Partition elimination based on expressions |\n\n---\n\n## 6. Concurrency and Transaction Support\n\n### 6.1 Part State Machine\n\n```\nPart Lifecycle:\n\n  1. Temporary (tmp_*)\n     ├─ Being written by MergeTreeDataWriter\n     └─ Not visible to queries\n\n  2. PreActive\n     ├─ Part directory renamed to final name\n     ├─ But not yet in ActiveDataPartSet\n     └─ Mutation processing in progress\n\n  3. Active\n     ├─ In ActiveDataPartSet\n     ├─ Visible to all new queries\n     └─ Can be merged with other parts\n\n  4. Outdated\n     ├─ Covered by another part (contains() returns true)\n     ├─ Still visible to existing transactions\n     └─ Can be safely deleted after transaction cleanup\n\n  5. Deleting\n     ├─ Removal in progress\n     └─ Clean up directory\n```\n\n### 6.2 Transaction Snapshot Isolation\n\n- **MVCC**: Transactions see consistent snapshot of parts at snapshot time\n- **Snapshot Creation**: Records active parts set at query start\n- **Part Visibility**: Part visible if created before transaction snapshot time\n- **Outdated Part Retention**: Parts kept until all transactions using them complete\n\n### 6.3 Thread Safety\n\n- **data_parts_mutex**: RWLock protecting ActiveDataPartSet modifications\n- **DataPartsLock**: Scoped lock wrapper with timing instrumentation\n- **Atomic Operations**: Part state transitions use atomics\n- **No Copy-on-Write**: Parts are immutable once active\n\n---\n\n## 7. File Reference Summary\n\n### Core Infrastructure (10+ files referenced)\n\n1. **MergeTreeData.h** - Central orchestrator, part set management, metadata storage\n2. **IMergeTreeDataPart.h** - Abstract base class for all data parts\n3. **MergeTreePartInfo.h** - Part naming, hierarchical containment logic\n4. **MergeTreeDataWriter.h** - INSERT path, partition splitting, temp part creation\n5. **MergedBlockOutputStream.h** - Column serialization, index building, compression\n6. **MergeTreeDataMergerMutator.h** - Merge selection, part picking heuristics\n7. **MergeTask.h** - Core merge execution, horizontal/vertical stages\n8. **MergeTreeDataSelectExecutor.h** - Query planning, part/mark selection\n9. **KeyCondition.h** - Primary key index evaluation, RPN condition\n10. **IMergeTreeReader.h** - Reader abstraction for column data retrieval\n11. **MergeTreeReaderWide.h** - Wide format reader with per-column streams\n12. **MergeTreeIndices.h** - Skip index interface and implementations\n13. **FutureMergedMutatedPart.h** - Merge specification and part naming\n14. **ActiveDataPartSet.h** - Active parts set with containment tracking\n15. **MergeList.h** - Merge progress monitoring and statistics\n\n### Supporting Components\n\n- **MergeTreeDataPartBuilder.h** - Part construction with metadata\n- **MergeTreeDataPartWide.h** - Wide format part implementation\n- **MergeTreeDataPartCompact.h** - Compact format part implementation\n- **MergeTreeReaderCompact.h** - Compact format reader\n- **MergeTreeReaderStream.h** - Single column read buffer with marks\n- **MergeTreeIOSettings.h** - Read/write buffer settings\n- **PartitionPruner.h** - Partition elimination\n- **RangesInDataPart.h** - Mark range management\n\n---\n\n## 8. Optimization Techniques\n\n### 8.1 Sparse Indexing\n- **Primary Key Index**: Stores only ~1/granule_size samples\n- **Storage**: Compressed in `primary.idx` file (~KB for large parts)\n- **Efficacy**: Can eliminate 90%+ of data before reading\n\n### 8.2 Adaptive Granularity\n- **Goal**: Keep granule sizes consistent despite variable row sizes\n- **Implementation**: `MergeTreeIndexGranularityAdaptive`\n- **Benefit**: Predictable I/O patterns, better parallelism\n\n### 8.3 Mark Caching\n- **Component**: `MarkCache` in-memory cache\n- **Content**: Decompressed mark ranges\n- **Hit Rate**: High for repeated scans of same columns\n- **Size Control**: Configurable via `mark_cache_size_ratio`\n\n### 8.4 Parallel Column Reading\n- **Wide Format**: Each column can read independently\n- **Threads**: Configurable thread pool for parallel reads\n- **Scheduling**: Dynamic task scheduling based on part size\n\n### 8.5 Projection Materialization\n- **Concept**: Pre-computed tables with different key structure\n- **Usage**: Automatic selection when projection query is subset of main table query\n- **Merge**: Built automatically during main part merges\n\n---\n\n## 9. Performance Characteristics\n\n### Write Performance\n- **Sequential**: Parts written sequentially to disk with compression\n- **Throughput**: Limited by compression speed and disk I/O\n- **Latency**: Sorting + compression + index building (typically 100ms-10s depending on size)\n\n### Merge Performance\n- **Throughput**: Merges are I/O bound, parallelism from multiple merges\n- **CPU Efficiency**: Merge algorithm is cache-friendly (sequential scan)\n- **Space**: Requires 2x source size temporarily for merge output\n- **Impact**: Background merges should not block inserts (async execution)\n\n### Query Performance\n- **Partition Pruning**: O(1) hash lookup of partitions\n- **Index Range Finding**: O(log n) binary search in primary key\n- **Skip Index Filtering**: O(num_granules) evaluation\n- **Column Reading**: Proportional to marks selected, parallelized\n\n---\n\n## 10. Edge Cases and Special Handling\n\n### 10.1 Mutation Processing\n- **Lazy Application**: Mutations create entries but don't modify parts immediately\n- **Query Application**: Readers apply mutations on-the-fly from mutation log\n- **Background Application**: Merge task creates new part with mutations applied\n- **Versioning**: Each part knows which mutations it contains\n\n### 10.2 TTL (Time-to-Live)\n- **TTL Index**: Tracks min/max TTL expression value per granule\n- **Cleanup**: Removes expired rows during merges\n- **Group By TTL**: Merge selector can prioritize parts with expired data\n\n### 10.3 Replicated MergeTree\n- **ZooKeeper**: Coordination of merges across replicas\n- **Merge Log**: Entries in ZooKeeper ensure consistent merge ordering\n- **Leader Election**: First replica to claim merge executes it\n- **Data Exchange**: Other replicas can fetch merged part directly\n\n### 10.4 Compact Format Selection\n- **Threshold 1**: `min_bytes_for_wide_part` (default 10MB)\n- **Threshold 2**: `min_rows_for_wide_part` (default 1M rows)\n- **Decision**: Use compact if both size AND row count below thresholds\n- **Conversion**: Automatic during merge if size grows\n\n---\n\n## 11. Key Design Insights\n\n1. **Immutability**: Parts never change once committed, enabling simple concurrency\n2. **Append-Only**: Inserts create new parts, no in-place updates or deletes\n3. **Lazy Merging**: Merges happen asynchronously in background with priority queue\n4. **Sparse Indexing**: Primary key index is ~0.1-1% of data size but can eliminate 90%+ of scans\n5. **Format Flexibility**: Wide format for analytical queries, compact format for write efficiency\n6. **Projection Materialization**: Pre-compute alternative indices automatically\n7. **Coroutine Merges**: Merges implemented as suspendable tasks, not blocking threads\n8. **MVCC Visibility**: Transactions see consistent part set snapshot independent of concurrent changes\n\n---\n\n## Conclusion\n\nThe MergeTree storage engine combines several key innovations:\n- **Columnar + Sorted**: Column-oriented storage with sorted primary key enables both compression and efficient range queries\n- **Hierarchical Merging**: Background merges consolidate small parts into larger ones, balancing write latency and merge cost\n- **Sparse + Layered Indexing**: Multi-level indices (primary key + skip indices) prune data efficiently\n- **Immutable Architecture**: Append-only design eliminates locking complexity and enables simple MVCC\n\nThis architecture makes ClickHouse exceptionally fast for analytical workloads with high insert throughput and complex ad-hoc queries over billions of rows.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":12,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2511,"cache_read_input_tokens":67850,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":2511,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":4,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"49ea5708-59c1-496e-bb6c-b21d20462f41","uuid":"79250872-7c6a-4916-92e4-97263eaa3056"}

It uses an append-only design where new data is written as immutable sorted parts, with background merge processes combining parts over time. The architecture is optimized for analytics workloads with column-oriented storage, sophisticated indexing, and intelligent query optimization.\n\n---\n\n## 1. High-Level Architecture Diagram\n\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│                     ClickHouse MergeTree                        │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n\n┌─────────────────┐  ┌──────────────────┐  ┌──────────────────────┐\n│   INSERT Path   │  │    MERGE Path    │  │     SELECT Path      │\n│                 │  │                  │  │                      │\n│ ┌─────────────┐ │  │ ┌──────────────┐ │  │ ┌──────────────────┐ │\n│ │  Parser &   │ │  │ │  Merge       │ │  │ │ Query Planner    │ │\n│ │  Optimizer  │ │  │ │  Selector    │ │  │ │ & Optimizer      │ │\n│ └──────┬──────┘ │  │ └──────┬───────┘ │  │ └────────┬─────────┘ │\n│        │        │  │        │         │  │         │           │\n│ ┌──────▼──────┐ │  │ ┌──────▼──────┐ │  │ ┌───────▼──────────┐ │\n│ │  Data       │ │  │ │  Merge      │ │  │ │ Partition &      │ │\n│ │  Writer    │ │  │ │  Algorithm  │ │  │ │ Mark Pruning     │ │\n│ └──────┬──────┘ │  │ └──────┬──────┘ │  │ └────────┬─────────┘ │\n│        │        │  │        │        │  │         │           │\n└────────┼────────┘  └────────┼────────┘  └─────────┼───────────┘\n         │                    │                     │\n         │                    │                     │\n      ┌──▼────────────────────▼─────────────────────▼────┐\n      │        Active Data Part Set (Metadata)            │\n      │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │\n      │  │  Part Info: partition_id, min/max_block,    │ │\n      │  │  level, mutation version                    │ │\n      │  │  • Controls which parts are visible         │ │\n      │  │  • Tracks part hierarchy and versioning    │ │\n      │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │\n      └──┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n         │\n    ┌────▼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n    │                    Data Parts on Disk                        │\n    │                                                               │\n    │  Part 1: [partition_0_1_5_2]  Part 2: [partition_0_6_10_2] │\n    │  ┌──────────────────────────┐  ┌──────────────────────────┐ │\n    │  │ columns.txt              │  │ columns.txt              │ │\n    │  │ primary.idx              │  │ primary.idx              │ │\n    │  │ skp_idx_*.idx (indices)  │  │ skp_idx_*.idx (indices)  │ │\n    │  │ column1.bin/mrk*         │  │ column1.bin/mrk*         │ │\n    │  │ column2.bin/mrk*         │  │ column2.bin/mrk*         │ │\n    │  │ checksums.txt            │  │ checksums.txt            │ │\n    │  │ partition.dat            │  │ partition.dat            │ │\n    │  └──────────────────────────┘  └──────────────────────────┘ │\n    │                                                               │\n    └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n---\n\n## 2. Core Architecture Components\n\n### 2.1 MergeTreeData - Central Orchestrator\n**File**: `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeData.h`\n\nMergeTreeData is the central class managing the entire storage engine:\n\n- **DataPartSet Management**: Maintains active parts with `ActiveDataPartSet` using a sorted map indexed by `MergeTreePartInfo`\n- **Metadata**: Stores table schema, partitioning expression, primary key, and index definitions\n- **Transaction Support**: Manages MVCC with transaction snapshots for consistent reads\n- **Mutex Hierarchy**: Thread-safe part management with `data_parts_mutex`\n- **Background Jobs**: Coordinates merge and mutation tasks through `BackgroundJobsAssignee`\n\n### 2.2 IMergeTreeDataPart - Part Abstraction\n**File**: `src/Storages/MergeTree/IMergeTreeDataPart.h`\n\nBase class for all data part implementations:\n\n- **Part Identity**: Name (e.g., `partition_0_1_5_2`), UUID, modification time\n- **Metadata**: Column list, checksums, index granularity information\n- **State Tracking**: Part state machine (PreActive → Active → Outdated → Deleting)\n- **Reader Factory**: Creates readers for specific column/mark ranges\n- **Concrete Subclasses**:\n  - `MergeTreeDataPartWide`: Wide format (one file per column) - optimal for large parts\n  - `MergeTreeDataPartCompact`: Compact format (all columns in one file) - optimal for small parts\n\n### 2.3 MergeTreePartInfo - Part Naming and Hierarchyy\n**File**: `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreePartInfo.h`\n\nEncodes part hierarchy in its name: `partition_id_min_block_max_block_level_mutation`\n\n- **Partition ID**: Logical partition identifier (e.g., date, month)\n- **Block Range**: [min_block, max_block] identifies rows by generation number\n- **Level**: Merge depth (0=initial part, 1=merged 2 parts, 2+=merged multiple times)\n- **Mutation**: Version number if part contains mutations\n- **Containment Logic**: Method `contains()` determines if one part fully contains another based on block ranges and levels\n\n---\n\n## 3. Write Path: INSERT to Committed Part\n\n### 3.1 Process Flow\n\n```\nINSERT query\n    ↓\nMergeTreeSink (Sink.h)\n    ↓\nMergeTreeDataWriter::splitBlockIntoParts\n    ├─ Partition pruning by partitioning expression\n    ├─ Group rows into blocks per partition\n    └─ Create BlocksWithPartition vector\n    ↓\nMergeTreeDataWriter::writeTempPart\n    ├─ Generate temporary part name (tmp_...)\n    ├─ Create part directory structure\n    ├─ Allocate disk space reservation\n    └─ Build part using MergeTreeDataPartBuilder\n    ↓\nMergedBlockOutputStream (Wide/Compact format)\n    ├─ Write column data in blocks (granules)\n    │   ├─ Column serialization per granule\n    │   └─ Compression applied\n    ├─ Write marks files (data.mrk*, *.mrk)\n    │   └─ Track seek positions for granule boundaries\n    ├─ Build primary index (primary.idx)\n    │   └─ Sample primary key values at mark boundaries\n    └─ Build skip indices (skp_idx_*.idx)\n    ↓\nMetadata Finalization\n    ├─ Compute and write column checksums (checksums.txt)\n    ├─ Write columns manifest (columns.txt)\n    ├─ Write partition value if custom partitioning (partition.dat)\n    └─ Write index granularity info\n    ↓\nCommit to Active Set\n    ├─ Rename tmp_* → partition_min_max_0\n    ├─ Add to ActiveDataPartSet\n    ├─ Update transaction metadata\n    └─ Notify background processes of new part\n    ↓\nTask Complete\n```\n\n### 3.2 Key Classes and Files\n\n| File Path | Role |\n|-----------|------|\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeDataWriter.h` | Main write orchestrator, partition splitting |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeDataWriter.cpp` | Write implementation, temp part creation |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergedBlockOutputStream.h` | Column data and index writing for Wide parts |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergedBlockOutputStream.cpp` | Stream buffer management and compression |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeSink.h` | INSERT sink interface connecting parser to writer |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeDataPartBuilder.h` | Part object construction with metadata |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeDataPartBuilder.cpp` | Builder implementation and part type selection |\n\n### 3.3 Data Part Structure on Disk\n\n**Wide Format (for large parts)**:\n```\npartition_0_1_5_2/\n├── columns.txt              # List of all columns and their types\n├── primary.idx              # Primary key index (compact, sampled at marks)\n├── skp_idx_name1.idx        # Skip index for index \"name1\"\n├── skp_idx_name2.idx        # Skip index for index \"name2\"\n├── column_name1.bin         # Compressed column data\n├── column_name1.mrk3        # Marks for column_name1\n├── column_name2.bin         # Compressed column data\n├── column_name2.mrk3        # Marks for column_name2\n├── checksums.txt            # File checksums for integrity verification\n├── partition.dat            # Partition expression value (optional)\n└── count.txt               # Total row count\n\nMark file (.mrk3) contains:\n  - Compressed block offsets (8 bytes each)\n  - Uncompressed block offsets (8 bytes each)\n  - Row count in granule (4 bytes)\n  For each granule boundary\n```\n\n**Compact Format (for small parts, <10MB)**:\n```\npartition_0_1_5_2/\n├── columns.txt\n├── primary.idx\n├── data.bin                 # All columns in single file\n├── data.mrk3               # Array of marks for all columns\n├── checksums.txt\n└── count.txt\n```\n\n### 3.4 Marks and Granularity\n\n- **Granule**: Logical unit of data (typically 8192 rows in non-adaptive mode)\n- **Mark**: Pair of offsets (compressed position in .bin file, uncompressed position in memory)\n- **Adaptive Granularity**: Adjusts granule size based on row size to maintain consistent granule sizes in bytes\n- **Mark Range**: [mark_id_start, mark_id_end] specifies which granules to read\n\n---\n\n## 4. Merge Path: Combining Parts\n\n### 4.1 Merge Selection Process\n\n```\nBackground Scheduler (MergeTreeBackgroundExecutor)\n    ↓\nMergeTreeDataMergerMutator::selectPartsToMerge\n    ├─ Step 1: Get Candidate Parts\n    │   └─ ActiveDataPartSet::getDataPartsToSelectMergeFrom()\n    │       Filters to visible, non-locked parts\n    │\n    ├─ Step 2: Determine Mergeable Ranges\n    │   └─ getPossibleMergeRanges()\n    │       Groups consecutive parts by partition\n    │       Checks can_merge_callback for conflicts\n    │\n    └─ Step 3: Select Optimal Parts\n        └─ selectPartsToMergeFromRanges()\n            Uses merge selector strategy:\n            ├─ Simple: Merge smallest adjacent parts\n            ├─ TTL-based: Prioritize parts with expired TTL\n            ├─ Balanced: Balance part count and size\n            └─ Graphite: Coarsen time-series data\n\nDecision: SelectPartsDecision (SELECTED | CANNOT_SELECT | NOTHING_TO_MERGE)\n```\n\n### 4.2 FutureMergedMutatedPart - Merge Task Specification\n**File**: `src/Storages/MergeTree/FutureMergedMutatedPart.h`\n\nDescribes the merge operation to perform:\n\n- **Source Parts**: `DataPartsVector parts` - ordered list of parts to merge\n- **Result Info**: `name`, `part_info` with new min/max blocks and incremented `level`\n- **Merge Type**: Regular merge vs. mutation vs. TTL cleanup\n- **Blocking Parts**: Parts that can be removed after merge completes\n\n### 4.3 Merge Execution - MergeTask\n**File**: `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTask.h`\n\nExecutes the actual merge as a coroutine-like task:\n\n```\nMergeTask::execute() (called repeatedly until returns false)\n    ├─ Stage 1: Preparation\n    │   ├─ Create output part directory structure\n    │   ├─ Prepare readers for all input parts\n    │   ├─ Setup merge algorithm (column-wise or block-wise)\n    │   └─ Initialize progress tracking\n    │\n    ├─ Stage 2: Horizontal Merge (combine row sets)\n    │   ├─ Read rows from input parts in sorted order\n    │   ├─ Apply merging logic (e.g., collapse, replace, sum)\n    │   ├─ Handle mutations if needed\n    │   ├─ Write output blocks via MergedBlockOutputStream\n    │   └─ Update merge progress\n    │\n    ├─ Stage 3: Vertical Merge (rebuild indices)\n    │   ├─ Rebuild primary index from merged data\n    │   ├─ Rebuild skip indices\n    │   └─ Verify data integrity with checksums\n    │\n    └─ Stage 4: Commit Merge\n        ├─ Finalize output part (checksums, columns.txt)\n        ├─ Rename from tmp_* to final name\n        ├─ Add to ActiveDataPartSet\n        ├─ Remove source parts\n        └─ Clean up temporary files\n```\n\n### 4.4 Merge Modes (MergeTreeData::MergingParams)\n\n| Mode | Behavior |\n|------|----------|\n| **Ordinary** | Simple concatenation and re-sorting |\n| **Replacing** | Keep only latest row per primary key (by version or insert order) |\n| **Collapsing** | Remove pairs of rows with opposite `sign` column values |\n| **Summing** | Sum numeric non-key columns for each primary key |\n| **Aggregating** | Merge pre-computed aggregate states |\n| **Graphite** | Coarsen time-series data for monitoring systems |\n\n### 4.5 Key Classes and Files\n\n| File Path | Role |\n|-----------|------|\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeDataMergerMutator.h` | Merge selection logic and heuristics |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeDataMergerMutator.cpp` | Implementation of merge selector |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTask.h` | Core merge execution task (coroutine-like) |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTask.cpp` | Merge algorithm stages: horizontal, vertical, commit |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeList.h` | Merge progress tracking and monitoring |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/ActiveDataPartSet.h` | Maintains set of active parts with containment logic |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/ActiveDataPartSet.cpp` | Part set operations and deduplication |\n\n---\n\n## 5. Read Path: SELECT Query Execution\n\n### 5.1 Query Planning and Optimization\n\n```\nSELECT query\n    ↓\nMergeTreeDataSelectExecutor::read()\n    ├─ Create ReadFromMergeTree processor\n    ├─ Select parts covering requested range\n    └─ Return QueryPlan with optimized execution\n    ↓\nPart Pruning (Partition Elimination)\n    ├─ Use partition expressions to eliminate partitions\n    ├─ PartitionPruner evaluates where conditions\n    └─ Keep only matching partitions\n    ↓\nPrimary Key Index Filtering\n    ├─ Convert WHERE clause to KeyCondition\n    ├─ KeyCondition::checkInRange() on part min/max values\n    └─ Determine mark ranges to read per part\n    ↓\nSkip Index Filtering (Secondary Indices)\n    ├─ Evaluate index conditions per granule\n    ├─ Uses IMergeTreeIndexCondition\n    ├─ Types: minmax, bloom filter, set, full-text, etc.\n    └─ Further refine mark ranges\n    ↓\nMark Range Selection\n    ├─ Merge adjacent/overlapping mark ranges\n    ├─ Determine which granules to actually read\n    └─ Pass to reader for column retrieval\n```\n\n### 5.2 MergeTreeDataSelectExecutor\n**File**: `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeDataSelectExecutor.h`\n\nMain query executor:\n\n- **Part Selection**: `read()` chooses which parts to scan based on constraints\n- **Index Application**: `markRangesFromPKRange()` converts primary key condition to granule ranges\n- **Multi-strategy**: Supports parallel reading, in-order reading, and parallel replicas\n- **Analysis Methods**: `estimateNumMarksToRead()` for cost estimation and projection selection\n\n### 5.3 KeyCondition - Primary Key Index\n**File**: `src/Storages/MergeTree/KeyCondition.h`\n\nEvaluates WHERE conditions against primary key ranges:\n\n- **RPN Compilation**: Converts condition DAG to Reverse Polish Notation\n- **Range Checking**: `checkInRange()` evaluates feasibility over [left_key, right_key] ranges\n- **Mask Result**: Returns `BoolMask` with (can_be_true, can_be_false) components\n- **Optimization**: Short-circuit evaluation when condition is always true/false\n- **Limitation**: Only uses prefix of primary key that can short-circuit the condition\n\n### 5.4 Skip Indices - Secondary Index Selection\n**File**: `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeIndices.h`\n\nPluggable secondary indices for further granule filtering:\n\n| Index Type | Purpose | Overhead |\n|-----------|---------|----------|\n| **minmax** | Track min/max per granule | Minimal (always built) |\n| **bloom_filter** | Fast membership testing | Medium (3-4% space) |\n| **set** | Enumerate allowed values | Low (hash set) |\n| **full-text** | Full-text search in text columns | High (inverted index) |\n| **similarity** | Vector similarity search | Very high (graph index) |\n\nIndex granule = one granule of data with one index entry\n\n### 5.5 Reader Interface - Column Data Retrieval\n**File**: `src/Storages/MergeTree/IMergeTreeReader.h`\n\nAbstract reader interface:\n\n```cpp\nreadRows(size_t from_mark, size_t current_task_last_mark,\n         bool continue_reading, size_t max_rows_to_read,\n         Columns & res_columns)\n  ├─ from_mark: starting granule ID\n  ├─ current_task_last_mark: prefetch boundary for async reads\n  ├─ continue_reading: resume from last position or seek\n  └─ max_rows_to_read: limit rows returned\n```\n\nConcrete implementations:\n- **MergeTreeReaderWide**: Reads Wide format (one file per column)\n- **MergeTreeReaderCompact**: Reads Compact format (all columns in one file)\n\n### 5.6 MergeTreeReaderWide - Wide Format Reading\n**File**: `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeReaderWide.h`\n\nOptimized reader for Wide parts:\n\n- **Per-Column Streams**: One `MergeTreeReaderStream` per column\n- **Parallel Column Loading**: Load multiple columns concurrently\n- **Mark Caching**: Cache decompressed mark ranges in memory\n- **Prefetching**: Async prefetch upcoming granules for remote storage\n\nReading process:\n```\nreadRows()\n  ├─ Seek each column's stream to from_mark\n  ├─ Deserialize column data for max_rows_to_read rows\n  ├─ Apply alter conversions if schema changed\n  ├─ Fill missing columns with defaults\n  └─ Return Columns vector\n```\n\n### 5.7 Data Access Hierarchy\n\n```\nColumn Data on Disk (.bin file)\n  ├─ Compression wrapper (e.g., LZ4, ZSTD)\n  ├─ Serialized blocks (per granule)\n  │   ├─ Block header (sizes, counts)\n  │   └─ Serialized values\n  └─ Footer (checksums)\n\nMark File (.mrk3) - Index into .bin\n  ├─ Entry 0: offset to granule 0\n  ├─ Entry 1: offset to granule 1\n  └─ Entry n: offset to granule n\n\nRead Process:\n  1. Load mark file into MarkCache\n  2. Seek to offset for from_mark\n  3. Stream decompression buffer\n  4. Deserialize rows until max_rows_to_read\n```\n\n### 5.8 Key Classes and Files\n\n| File Path | Role |\n|-----------|------|\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeDataSelectExecutor.h` | Query planning and part selection |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeDataSelectExecutor.cpp` | Index filtering and mark range calculation |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/KeyCondition.h` | Primary key condition evaluation |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/KeyCondition.cpp` | RPN compilation and range checking |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/IMergeTreeReader.h` | Reader interface abstraction |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeReaderWide.h` | Wide format reader implementation |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeReaderWide.cpp` | Column-by-column reading logic |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeReaderStream.h` | Single column read buffer with mark caching |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/MergeTreeIndices.h` | Skip index interface and implementations |\n| `src/Storages/MergeTree/PartitionPruner.h` | Partition elimination based on expressions |\n\n---\n\n## 6. Concurrency and Transaction Support\n\n### 6.1 Part State Machine\n\n```\nPart Lifecycle:\n\n  1. Temporary (tmp_*)\n     ├─ Being written by MergeTreeDataWriter\n     └─ Not visible to queries\n\n  2. PreActive\n     ├─ Part directory renamed to final name\n     ├─ But not yet in ActiveDataPartSet\n     └─ Mutation processing in progress\n\n  3. Active\n     ├─ In ActiveDataPartSet\n     ├─ Visible to all new queries\n     └─ Can be merged with other parts\n\n  4. Outdated\n     ├─ Covered by another part (contains() returns true)\n     ├─ Still visible to existing transactions\n     └─ Can be safely deleted after transaction cleanup\n\n  5. Deleting\n     ├─ Removal in progress\n     └─ Clean up directory\n```\n\n### 6.2 Transaction Snapshot Isolation\n\n- **MVCC**: Transactions see consistent snapshot of parts at snapshot time\n- **Snapshot Creation**: Records active parts set at query start\n- **Part Visibility**: Part visible if created before transaction snapshot time\n- **Outdated Part Retention**: Parts kept until all transactions using them complete\n\n### 6.3 Thread Safety\n\n- **data_parts_mutex**: RWLock protecting ActiveDataPartSet modifications\n- **DataPartsLock**: Scoped lock wrapper with timing instrumentation\n- **Atomic Operations**: Part state transitions use atomics\n- **No Copy-on-Write**: Parts are immutable once active\n\n---\n\n## 7. File Reference Summary\n\n### Core Infrastructure (10+ files referenced)\n\n1. **MergeTreeData.h** - Central orchestrator, part set management, metadata storage\n2. **IMergeTreeDataPart.h** - Abstract base class for all data parts\n3. **MergeTreePartInfo.h** - Part naming, hierarchical containment logic\n4. **MergeTreeDataWriter.h** - INSERT path, partition splitting, temp part creation\n5. **MergedBlockOutputStream.h** - Column serialization, index building, compression\n6. **MergeTreeDataMergerMutator.h** - Merge selection, part picking heuristics\n7. **MergeTask.h** - Core merge execution, horizontal/vertical stages\n8. **MergeTreeDataSelectExecutor.h** - Query planning, part/mark selection\n9. **KeyCondition.h** - Primary key index evaluation, RPN condition\n10. **IMergeTreeReader.h** - Reader abstraction for column data retrieval\n11. **MergeTreeReaderWide.h** - Wide format reader with per-column streams\n12. **MergeTreeIndices.h** - Skip index interface and implementations\n13. **FutureMergedMutatedPart.h** - Merge specification and part naming\n14. **ActiveDataPartSet.h** - Active parts set with containment tracking\n15. **MergeList.h** - Merge progress monitoring and statistics\n\n### Supporting Components\n\n- **MergeTreeDataPartBuilder.h** - Part construction with metadata\n- **MergeTreeDataPartWide.h** - Wide format part implementation\n- **MergeTreeDataPartCompact.h** - Compact format part implementation\n- **MergeTreeReaderCompact.h** - Compact format reader\n- **MergeTreeReaderStream.h** - Single column read buffer with marks\n- **MergeTreeIOSettings.h** - Read/write buffer settings\n- **PartitionPruner.h** - Partition elimination\n- **RangesInDataPart.h** - Mark range management\n\n---\n\n## 8. Optimization Techniques\n\n### 8.1 Sparse Indexing\n- **Primary Key Index**: Stores only ~1/granule_size samples\n- **Storage**: Compressed in `primary.idx` file (~KB for large parts)\n- **Efficacy**: Can eliminate 90%+ of data before reading\n\n### 8.2 Adaptive Granularity\n- **Goal**: Keep granule sizes consistent despite variable row sizes\n- **Implementation**: `MergeTreeIndexGranularityAdaptive`\n- **Benefit**: Predictable I/O patterns, better parallelism\n\n### 8.3 Mark Caching\n- **Component**: `MarkCache` in-memory cache\n- **Content**: Decompressed mark ranges\n- **Hit Rate**: High for repeated scans of same columns\n- **Size Control**: Configurable via `mark_cache_size_ratio`\n\n### 8.4 Parallel Column Reading\n- **Wide Format**: Each column can read independently\n- **Threads**: Configurable thread pool for parallel reads\n- **Scheduling**: Dynamic task scheduling based on part size\n\n### 8.5 Projection Materialization\n- **Concept**: Pre-computed tables with different key structure\n- **Usage**: Automatic selection when projection query is subset of main table query\n- **Merge**: Built automatically during main part merges\n\n---\n\n## 9. Performance Characteristics\n\n### Write Performance\n- **Sequential**: Parts written sequentially to disk with compression\n- **Throughput**: Limited by compression speed and disk I/O\n- **Latency**: Sorting + compression + index building (typically 100ms-10s depending on size)\n\n### Merge Performance\n- **Throughput**: Merges are I/O bound, parallelism from multiple merges\n- **CPU Efficiency**: Merge algorithm is cache-friendly (sequential scan)\n- **Space**: Requires 2x source size temporarily for merge output\n- **Impact**: Background merges should not block inserts (async execution)\n\n### Query Performance\n- **Partition Pruning**: O(1) hash lookup of partitions\n- **Index Range Finding**: O(log n) binary search in primary key\n- **Skip Index Filtering**: O(num_granules) evaluation\n- **Column Reading**: Proportional to marks selected, parallelized\n\n---\n\n## 10. Edge Cases and Special Handling\n\n### 10.1 Mutation Processing\n- **Lazy Application**: Mutations create entries but don't modify parts immediately\n- **Query Application**: Readers apply mutations on-the-fly from mutation log\n- **Background Application**: Merge task creates new part with mutations applied\n- **Versioning**: Each part knows which mutations it contains\n\n### 10.2 TTL (Time-to-Live)\n- **TTL Index**: Tracks min/max TTL expression value per granule\n- **Cleanup**: Removes expired rows during merges\n- **Group By TTL**: Merge selector can prioritize parts with expired data\n\n### 10.3 Replicated MergeTree\n- **ZooKeeper**: Coordination of merges across replicas\n- **Merge Log**: Entries in ZooKeeper ensure consistent merge ordering\n- **Leader Election**: First replica to claim merge executes it\n- **Data Exchange**: Other replicas can fetch merged part directly\n\n### 10.4 Compact Format Selection\n- **Threshold 1**: `min_bytes_for_wide_part` (default 10MB)\n- **Threshold 2**: `min_rows_for_wide_part` (default 1M rows)\n- **Decision**: Use compact if both size AND row count below thresholds\n- **Conversion**: Automatic during merge if size grows\n\n---\n\n## 11. Key Design Insights\n\n1. **Immutability**: Parts never change once committed, enabling simple concurrency\n2. **Append-Only**: Inserts create new parts, no in-place updates or deletes\n3. **Lazy Merging**: Merges happen asynchronously in background with priority queue\n4. **Sparse Indexing**: Primary key index is ~0.1-1% of data size but can eliminate 90%+ of scans\n5. **Format Flexibility**: Wide format for analytical queries, compact format for write efficiency\n6. **Projection Materialization**: Pre-compute alternative indices automatically\n7. **Coroutine Merges**: Merges implemented as suspendable tasks, not blocking threads\n8. **MVCC Visibility**: Transactions see consistent part set snapshot independent of concurrent changes\n\n---\n\n## Conclusion\n\nThe MergeTree storage engine combines several key innovations:\n- **Columnar + Sorted**: Column-oriented storage with sorted primary key enables both compression and efficient range queries\n- **Hierarchical Merging**: Background merges consolidate small parts into larger ones, balancing write latency and merge cost\n- **Sparse + Layered Indexing**: Multi-level indices (primary key + skip indices) prune data efficiently\n- **Immutable Architecture**: Append-only design eliminates locking complexity and enables simple MVCC\n\nThis architecture makes ClickHouse exceptionally fast for analytical workloads with high insert throughput and complex ad-hoc queries over billions of rows.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

It uses an append-only design where new data is written as immutable sorted parts, with background merge processes combining parts over time. The architecture is optimized for analytics workloads with column-oriented storage, sophisticated indexing, and intelligent query optimization.\n     6→\n     7→---\n     8→\n     9→## 1.
